<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that seven is the critical dimension for the one-phase Bernoulli problem: every nonzero one-homogeneous global minimizer in dimensions at most six is flat, while a nonflat one-homogeneous global minimizer exists in dimension seven. It follows that the interior free boundary of a local minimizer is smooth in dimensions at most six. In dimension <i>n</i> ≥ 7, its singular set has Hausdorff dimension at most $`n-7`$, and this bound is sharp. In dimension seven, the singular set is locally finite.

- **claim-main-critical-dimension** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Every nonzero one-homogeneous global minimizer of <a href="#eq:energy" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:energy">[eq:energy]</a> in $\mathbb R^d$, $1\leq d\leq6$, is flat. There is a nonflat one-homogeneous global minimizer in $\mathbb R^7$. Consequently, $$d_*=7.$$
  - Explanation: Nonzero one-homogeneous global minimizers are flat half-space solutions in dimensions up to six. A nonflat one in dimension seven shows that seven is the least dimension admitting one.
- **claim-singular-set-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-singular-set)): Let $n\ge2$, let $D\subset\mathbb R^n$ be open, and let $u\in H^1_{\mathrm{loc}}(D)$ be a nonnegative local minimizer of the one-phase Bernoulli energy in $D$. Its interior free boundary is smooth outside $\operatorname{Sing}(u)$. This set is empty when $n\le6$, is locally finite when $n=7$, and satisfies $$\dim_{\mathrm H}\operatorname{Sing}(u)\le n-7\qquad(n\ge7).$$ For every $n\ge7$, a global minimizer attains this dimension bound.
  - Explanation: For a nonnegative local minimizer the free boundary is smooth off the singular set. That set is empty up to dimension six, locally finite in dimension seven, and of Hausdorff dimension at most n-7 beyond, and the bound is attained.
- **claim-regularity-blowups** (theorem, [source](#stmt-red-regularity)): Let $d\geq2$ and let $u\geq0$ be a local minimizer of the one-phase energy with volume coefficient $1$ in an open set $D\subset\mathbb R^d$.* 1. *There is a locally Lipschitz representative of $u$, and it is harmonic in $\{u>0\}$.* 2. *If $x_0\in D\cap\partial\{u>0\}$, every sequence $r_j\downarrow0$ has a subsequence for which $$\label{red:blowup-sequence} u_{x_0,r_j}(y):=\frac{u(x_0+r_jy)}{r_j}\longrightarrow U(y)$$ locally uniformly in $\mathbb R^d$. The limit is a nonzero, nonnegative, one-homogeneous global minimizer. Along a further subsequence the convergence is strong in $H^1_{\mathrm{loc}}$, and the positivity indicators converge in $L^1_{\mathrm{loc}}$.* 3. *If one such limit equals $(y\cdot e)_+$ with $\|e\|=1$, then near $x_0$ the free boundary is a smooth hypersurface and $\{u>0\}$ is exactly one of its sides. The function $u$ is smooth up to that hypersurface from the positive side, and $$\label{red:bernoulli} u=0,\qquad \nabla u=N_E \quad\hbox{on the free boundary},$$ where $N_E$ is the Euclidean unit normal pointing into $\{u>0\}$.
  - Explanation: A local minimizer has a locally Lipschitz representative, harmonic where positive. Blowups at free-boundary points are nonzero one-homogeneous global minimizers. A half-space blowup forces a smooth free boundary satisfying the Bernoulli condition.
- **claim-translation-invariance** (theorem, [source](#stmt-red-translation)): Let $u$ be a one-homogeneous global minimizer, let $0\ne x_0\in\partial\{u>0\}$, and let $U$ be any limit in <a href="#red:blowup-sequence" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="red:blowup-sequence">[red:blowup-sequence]</a>. Then $$U(y+t x_0)=U(y)\qquad(y\in\mathbb R^d,\ t\in\mathbb R).$$
  - Explanation: Blowups of a one-homogeneous global minimizer at a nonzero free-boundary point are invariant along the line through that point, which is the key to reducing dimension.
- **claim-factor-minimality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-red-factor-minimality)): Suppose $U(y,s)=v(y)$ is a global minimizer in $\mathbb R^k\times\mathbb R$, where $v\geq0$ belongs to $H^1_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb R^k)$. Then $v$ is a global minimizer in $\mathbb R^k$.
  - Explanation: If a global minimizer does not depend on one coordinate, the remaining factor is itself a global minimizer in one dimension fewer.
- **claim-smooth-original-link** (theorem, [source](#stmt-red-original-link)): Every point of $\partial\{u>0\}\setminus\{0\}$ is regular. Put $m_o=d-1$ and $g_o=u\|_{S^{m_o}}$. The set $\{g_o>0\}$ is nonempty and proper, has finitely many connected components, and their closures are pairwise disjoint compact smooth manifolds with boundary. On each such component $\Omega_o$, the restriction of $g_o$ is smooth up to the boundary from within $\Omega_o$ and satisfies $$\label{red:link-equations} \Delta_{S^{m_o}}g_o=-m_o g_o\quad\hbox{in }\Omega_o, \qquad g_o=0,\quad \nabla_{S^{m_o}}g_o=N_o \quad\hbox{on }\partial\Omega_o,$$ where $N_o$ is the inward spherical unit normal. In particular, $\partial\Omega_o\ne\varnothing$.
  - Explanation: For a hypothetical first nonflat minimizing cone, every free-boundary point away from the vertex is regular. The spherical positive set has finitely many components with disjoint smooth closures satisfying the link equations.
- **claim-nonflat-component** (theorem, [source](#stmt-red-nonflat-component)): There is a component $\Omega_o$ in Proposition <a href="#red:original-link" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="red:original-link">6</a> such that $$\label{red:original-hessian} T_o:=\nabla^2_{S^{m_o}}g_o+g_o\mathop{\mathrm{Id}}\not\equiv0 \quad\hbox{on }\Omega_o.$$ Here $\mathop{\mathrm{Id}}$ denotes the spherical metric, viewed as a symmetric two-tensor.
  - Explanation: Some component of the spherical positive set has a Hessian tensor that is not identically zero; otherwise the cone would be a single half-space.
- **claim-suspension-minimality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-red-suspension)): If $u\geq0$ is a global minimizer in $\mathbb R^k$, then $\widetilde u(x,s)=u(x)$ is a global minimizer in $\mathbb R^{k+1}$. If $u$ is the cone in <a href="#red:first-dimension" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="red:first-dimension">[red:first-dimension]</a> with $d=5$, the singular free-boundary set of $\widetilde u$ is exactly $\{0\}\times\mathbb R$. For the component chosen in Lemma <a href="#red:nonflat-component" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="red:nonflat-component">7</a>, the corresponding positive component on $S^5$ is $$\label{red:suspension-coordinates} \widetilde\Omega =\bigl\{\Phi(t,y):=(\sin t\,y,\cos t): 0<t<\pi,\ y\in\Omega_o\bigr\}, \qquad \widetilde g(\Phi(t,y))=\sin t\,g_o(y).$$ Its boundary consists of the smooth side $\Phi((0,\pi)\times\partial\Omega_o)$ and the two singular tips $p_+=(0,1)$ and $p_-=(0,-1)$.
  - Explanation: Adding a trivial coordinate preserves global minimality. In the five-dimensional case the singular set is the axis, and the suspended spherical component has two tips.
- **claim-interior-identities** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-interior-identities)): The tensor $T$ is symmetric and trace-free, and $B$ is fully symmetric and trace-free in every pair of slots. In particular $\mathop{\mathrm{div}}T=0$. The following identities hold: $$\begin{aligned} \nabla_lB_{ijk}-\nabla_iB_{ljk} &=\delta_{lj}T_{ik}+\delta_{lk}T_{ij} -\delta_{ij}T_{lk}-\delta_{ik}T_{lj}, \label{geo:commutator}\\ \Delta T&=mT, &\Delta w^2&=2m w^2+2\|B\|^2, \label{geo:bochner}\\ \nabla_l p_i&=T_{li}-g\delta_{li}, &\nabla_l g&=p_l, \label{geo:first-jets}\\ \nabla s^2&=2Tp, &\Delta s^2&=2w^2. \label{geo:s-identities} \end{aligned}$$ On the open set $\{w>0\}$ one also has $$\label{geo:w-gradient} \nabla_l w=w^{-1}T_{jk}B_{ljk},\qquad \|\nabla w\|\le \|B\|.$$ The inequality $\|\nabla(w^2)\|^2\le4w^2\|B\|^2$ holds everywhere, including at $w=0$.
  - Explanation: The spherical Hessian tensor T is symmetric, trace-free and divergence-free, and its derivative B is fully symmetric. These give the commutator, Laplacian and Bochner identities used throughout.
- **claim-strict-gradient-bound** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-gradient-bound)): On $\Omega_o$ one has $s_o^2<1$. At every boundary point, put $$\label{geo:H-definition} H_o=-T_o(N_o,N_o).$$ Then $$\label{geo:strict-boundary-sign} H_o>0,\qquad T_op_o=-H_op_o,\qquad w_o>0 \quad\hbox{on }\partial\Omega_o.$$ Consequently $w_o$ has a positive minimum on $\partial\Omega_o$, and its zero set is a compact subset of $\Omega_o$.
  - Explanation: On the original component the combined function-and-gradient quantity stays strictly below its boundary value. The boundary curvature is positive and the Hessian norm is positive on the boundary, so its zero set is compact in the interior.
- **claim-boundary-jets** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-boundary-jets)): Suppose $g=0$, $p=N$, and $T(N,N)=-H<0$ on a smooth boundary portion, where $N$ is inward. Let Greek indices be tangent to this boundary and put $$\mathfrak h_{\alpha\beta} =\langle\nabla_{\alpha}N,e_\beta\rangle.$$ Then $T_{N\alpha}=0$, $T_{\alpha\beta}=\mathfrak h_{\alpha\beta}$, and $H=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\mathfrak h$; in particular $H$ is a sum of principal curvatures with the specified convention, without division by $m-1$. If $\nabla^{\partial}$ is the boundary connection, all components of $B$ are described, up to permutation of slots, by $$\begin{aligned} B_{\alpha\beta\gamma} &=(\nabla^{\partial}_{\alpha}\mathfrak h)_{\beta\gamma}, &B_{NN\alpha}&=-\partial_\alpha H, \label{geo:even-boundary-jets}\\ B_{N\alpha\beta} &=T_{NN}T_{\alpha\beta}-(T^2)_{\alpha\beta}, &B_{NNN}&=w^2. \label{geo:odd-boundary-jets} \end{aligned}$$ The components with an odd number of normal slots are thus determined by the pointwise tensor $T$; the other components involve tangential derivatives of curvature. The tensor $V$ below encodes this odd-normal part after normalization. Here $w>0$. Define the normalized tensors and eigenvalue $$A=T/w,\qquad \lambda=T_{NN}/w=-H/w, \qquad S_0=\lambda A-A^2,$$ and the symmetric tensor $$\label{geo:boundary-V} V_{ijk}=p_i(S_0)_{jk}+p_j(S_0)_{ki}+p_k(S_0)_{ij} +p_ip_jp_k.$$ Then $\|p\|=1$, $Ap=\lambda p$, $\lambda<0$, $S_0p=0$, $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}S_0=-1$, and $V$ is trace-free. The tensors $B/w^2$ and $V$ agree in every component with an odd number of normal slots. Moreover, $$\label{geo:normal-w} V_{Njk}=(S_0+p\otimes p)_{jk},\qquad \partial_N w=w^2\bigl(2\lambda-\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}A^3\bigr).$$
  - Explanation: On the free boundary the components of the third-order tensor with an odd number of normal slots are determined by the Hessian tensor. The normalized tensor V encodes them and gives the normal derivative of w.
- **claim-suspension-data** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-suspension-data)): If $m_o=4$, write $\rho=\sin t$, $c=\cos t$ and parameterize the regular part of the suspended component by $$(t,y)\longmapsto (\rho y,c),\qquad 0<t<\pi,\quad y\in\Omega_o.$$ Its metric is $dt^2+\rho^2 ds_{S^4}^2$, and $\widetilde g=\rho g_o$. With $E_0=\partial_t$ and $E_\alpha=\rho^{-1}e_\alpha$ for a lifted orthonormal frame on $S^4$, the data satisfy $$\begin{aligned} \widetilde p_0&=c g_o, &\widetilde p_\alpha&=(p_o)_\alpha, &\widetilde s^2&=s_o^2, \label{geo:suspended-first-jets}\\ \widetilde T_{00}&=0, &\widetilde T_{0\alpha}&=0, &\widetilde T_{\alpha\beta}&=\rho^{-1}(T_o)_{\alpha\beta}. \label{geo:suspended-T} \end{aligned}$$ The only possibly nonzero components of $\widetilde B$ are $$\label{geo:suspended-B} \widetilde B_{\alpha\beta\gamma} =\rho^{-2}(B_o)_{\alpha\beta\gamma},\qquad \widetilde B_{0\alpha\beta} =\widetilde B_{\alpha0\beta} =\widetilde B_{\alpha\beta0} =-c\rho^{-2}(T_o)_{\alpha\beta}.$$ In particular, $$\label{geo:suspended-norms} \widetilde w=\rho^{-1}w_o,\qquad \|\widetilde B\|^2 =\rho^{-4}\bigl(\|B_o\|^2+3c^2w_o^2\bigr),\qquad \widetilde H=\rho^{-1}H_o \quad\hbox{on the regular side.}$$ The volume and side-boundary measures are $$\label{geo:suspended-measures} dV=\rho^4\,dt\,dV_o,\qquad d\sigma=\rho^3\,dt\,d\sigma_o.$$ Every local identity of Proposition <a href="#geo:interior-identities" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="geo:interior-identities">9</a> holds with $m=5$, and Proposition <a href="#geo:boundary-jets" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="geo:boundary-jets">11</a> holds on the regular side. Also $\widetilde s^2<1$ inside and $\widetilde H,\widetilde w>0$ on that side.
  - Explanation: In the suspended case the tensor data, norms, curvature and measures are given by explicit warped-product formulas in the base data and the suspension angle.
- **claim-euclidean-stability** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-euclidean-stability)): Let $D$ be the selected positivity component of a global minimizing cone in $\mathbb R^{m+1}$. Let $\phi$ be any real smooth function up to the regular boundary of $D$, with compact support in that regular closure away from the vertex and from any other singular points. Then $$\label{geo:euclidean-inequality} \int_D \|\nabla\phi\|^2\,dx \ge \int_{\partial D}H_E\phi^2\,d\sigma_E, \qquad H_E=-D^2u(N_E,N_E),$$ where $N_E$ is the inward Euclidean unit normal. No restriction is imposed on the boundary trace or normal derivative of $\phi$.
  - Explanation: Minimality gives a second-variation inequality on the positivity component of a cone: the Dirichlet energy of any test function dominates its boundary curvature term, with no condition on the boundary trace.
- **claim-spherical-stability** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-spherical-stability)): For every real function $v$ smooth up to the regular boundary of the working component $\Omega\subset S^5$, with compact support in its closure away from the tips if present, $$\label{geo:spherical-inequality} \int_{\partial\Omega}H v^2\,d\sigma \le \int_\Omega\bigl(\|\nabla v\|^2+4v^2\bigr)\,dV.$$ The trace of $v$ on the regular boundary is arbitrary.
  - Explanation: Separating the radial variable and using the sharp radial Hardy constant turns the Euclidean stability inequality into one on the spherical link, with an arbitrary boundary trace.
- **claim-universal-certificate** (bound, [source](#stmt-cert-universal)): There are rational tensor polynomials $f,U_{jk},K_{lijk},M_l,P_l$ in $A,p,g,\delta$, with the following properties. They are equivariant under orthogonal changes of coordinates, with $f$ invariant. Their coefficients are specified by Table I in Appendix <a href="#app:tables" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="app:tables">9</a>, using the decoding in Section <a href="#cert:decode" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cert:decode">6.1</a>. Put $$C_{lijk}=K_{lijk}-K_{iljk},\qquad G_{lijk}=\delta_{li}U_{jk}+C_{lijk}.$$ For every choice of the data above with $\chi\ge0$, $$\begin{aligned} \mathcal I:={}&z^2\left[5U_{jk}A_{jk} +C_{lijk}(\delta_{lj}A_{ik}+\delta_{lk}A_{ij})-4f\right]\notag\\ &+\left[D_lG_{lijk}+(2a-1)q_lG_{lijk}\right]X_{ijk}\notag\\ &+w\left[D_lM_l+(2a+1)q_lM_l\right] +z\left[D_lP_l+2aq_lP_l\right]\notag\\ &+f\|\ell\|^2-(D_lf+2aq_lf)\ell_l \ \ge\ \epsilon_*(z^2+\|\ell\|^2).\label{cert:interior} \end{aligned}$$ In particular, $f\ge\epsilon_*$ whenever $g^2+\|p\|^2\le1$.* *For every symmetric trace-free unit matrix $A$ and vector $p$ satisfying $$g=0,\qquad \|p\|=1,\qquad Ap=\lambda p,\qquad\lambda<0,$$ define $$S_0=\lambda A-A^2,\qquad V_{ijk}=p_i(S_0)_{jk}+p_j(S_0)_{ki}+p_k(S_0)_{ij}+p_ip_jp_k.$$ Then $$\label{cert:boundary} \mathcal S:=-\lambda f+U_{jk}(S_0+p\otimes p)_{jk} +C_{lijk}p_lV_{ijk}+p_lM_l \ \ge\ \delta_*.$$ Moreover, $U$ and $K$ are even in $p$, and $P=0$ when $g=0$.
  - Explanation: Fixed rational tensor polynomials satisfy an interior inequality with positive margin and a boundary inequality with positive margin, for all admissible free algebraic data.
- **claim-normalized-differentiation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-flux-normalized-differentiation)): At the data <a href="#flux:normalized-data" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="flux:normalized-data">[flux:normalized-data]</a>, the derivation $D_l$ of <a href="#cert:D" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cert:D">[cert:D]</a>, evaluated at $z=1$, equals covariant differentiation on every coefficient polynomial in $A,p,g,\delta$. In particular, $$\label{flux:first-derivatives} \nabla_lw=wq_l,\qquad \nabla_lA_{ij}=X_{lij}-q_lA_{ij},\qquad \nabla_lp_i=wA_{li}-g\delta_{li},\qquad \nabla_lg=p_l.$$ Furthermore, $$\label{flux:fourth-elimination} \frac1wG_{lijk}\nabla_lB_{ijk} =5U_{jk}A_{jk} +C_{lijk}(\delta_{lj}A_{ik}+\delta_{lk}A_{ij}).$$
  - Explanation: The formal derivation, evaluated at the geometric data, coincides with covariant differentiation, and the contraction identities remove all fourth derivatives of g.
- **claim-interior-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-flux-interior-comparison)): The scalar $h$ and vector field $Z$ satisfy $$\label{flux:interior-inequality} \mathop{\mathrm{div}}Z\geq 4h^2+\|\nabla h\|^2+\epsilon_*w^{2a} \qquad\text{on }\Omega_+.$$
  - Explanation: The divergence of the vector field Z dominates the stability energy density of the scalar h plus a positive multiple of a power of w, on the set where the Hessian norm is positive.
- **claim-boundary-parity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-flux-boundary-parity)): At such a point, $$\label{flux:boundary-contraction} p_lG_{lijk}\frac{B_{ijk}}{w^2} =p_lG_{lijk}V_{ijk} =U_{jk}(S_0+p\otimes p)_{jk}+C_{lijk}p_lV_{ijk}.$$
  - Explanation: Reflection symmetry in the normal direction kills the even-normal jet components under contraction, so the boundary contraction is computed from the odd-normal part alone.
- **claim-boundary-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-flux-boundary-comparison)): On the regular side boundary, $$\label{flux:signed-boundary-identity} N\cdot Z+Hh^2=w^{2a+1}\mathcal S \geq\delta_*w^{2a+1}>0, \qquad \delta_* =\frac{6984137359}{31250000000},$$ where $\mathcal S$ is the boundary polynomial in <a href="#cert:boundary" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cert:boundary">[cert:boundary]</a>. In particular $N\cdot Z+Hh^2\geq0$.
  - Explanation: On the regular side boundary the normal flux of Z plus the curvature term is bounded below by a strictly positive multiple of a power of w.
- **claim-field-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-flux-field-bounds)): There is a finite constant $C$, depending only on the coefficient polynomials, the dimension, $a$, and $\epsilon_*$, such that on $\Omega_+$ $$\label{flux:uniform-field-bounds} \|h\|\leq Cw^a,\qquad \|\nabla h\|\leq Cw^a(1+w+\|X\|),\qquad \|Z\|\leq Cw^{2a}(1+w+\|X\|).$$ The constant is independent of $w$, $\|X\|$, and all subsequent cutoff parameters.
  - Explanation: The scalar, its gradient and the vector field are bounded by powers of w times the size of the normalized derivative tensor, with a constant independent of the later cutoff parameters.
- **claim-positive-projections** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cert-positive-projections)): All these operators are self-adjoint and positive semidefinite on their entire tensor spaces. In particular, $\Gamma\ge0$ whenever $\chi\ge0$.
  - Explanation: The scaled projection operators in the Gram blocks are self-adjoint and positive semidefinite, so the comparison form is nonnegative whenever its weight is.
- **claim-graph-closure** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cert-closure)): The preceding rules evaluate all terms of $\mathcal I$ and $\Gamma$ by a finite sum of scalar graphs. In every intermediate graph a free slot has exactly one incidence, each cubic tensor has exactly three incidences, and there are at most two cubic tensors. Every resulting scalar graph has degree two in $(w,z,\ell,X)$.
  - Explanation: The contraction and differentiation rules reduce every term to a finite sum of scalar graphs, each of degree two in the free variables and with at most two cubic tensors.
- **claim-tensor-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-cert-tensor-bounds)): For the constrained matrix $A$ and symmetric trace-free tensor $X$, $$\begin{aligned} \\|A\\|_{\mathrm{op}}^2&\le\theta,& \|A^k\|^2&\le\theta^{k-1}\quad(k\ge1),\label{cert:matrix-bounds}\\ \|\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}A^k\|^2&\le\theta^{k-2}\quad(k\ge2),& \|\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}A^3\|^2&\le\frac9{20},\label{cert:trace-bounds}\\ \|X:A\|^2&\le\frac57\|X\|^2,& (X:A)_i&=X_{ijk}A_{jk}.\label{cert:five-sevenths} \end{aligned}$$ If $h,b>0$ and $g^2+\|p\|^2\le1$, then $$\label{cert:mixed-bound} \|g\|^{2h}\|p\|^{2b} \le \left(\frac{h}{h+b}\right)^h \left(\frac{b}{h+b}\right)^b.$$
  - Explanation: Sharp operator, Frobenius, cubic-trace and contraction bounds hold for constrained matrices and symmetric trace-free tensors, together with a mixed bound for powers of g and the norm of p.
- **claim-graph-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-cert-graph-bound)): Every scalar graph retained by Lemma <a href="#cert:closure" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cert:closure">22</a> satisfies $$\label{cert:monomial-bound} \|\mathcal M(t)\|\le L_t y_i y_j$$ on $g^2+\|p\|^2\le1$.
  - Explanation: Every retained scalar graph is bounded by an explicit rational factor times a product of two magnitudes, by splitting into components and using the tensor bounds.
- **claim-weighted-bochner** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cut-weighted-bochner)): The set $\mathcal Z_o$ is a compact subset of $\Omega_o$, and $$\label{cut:weighted-integrability} \int_{\Omega_{o,+}}w_o^{2a-2}\|B_o\|^2\,dV_o<\infty.$$ Consequently the function $$\label{cut:original-dominator} D_o(y)= \begin{cases} w_o(y)^{2a}\bigl(1+\|B_o(y)\|/w_o(y)\bigr)^2, &y\in\Omega_{o,+},\\ 0,&y\in\mathcal Z_o, \end{cases}$$ belongs to $L^1(\Omega_o)$.
  - Explanation: The Hessian zero set is compact inside the original component and a weighted integral of the derivative tensor is finite, so the dominating function is integrable.
- **claim-integrated-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-cut-integrated-comparison)): For each fixed $j\geq3$, $$\label{cut:three-errors} \epsilon_*\int_\Omega\xi_j^2w^{2a}\,dV \leq\int_\Omega\left( h^2\|\nabla\xi_j\|^2 +2\xi_jh\langle\nabla h,\nabla\xi_j\rangle -2\xi_j\langle Z,\nabla\xi_j\rangle\right)\,dV.$$
  - Explanation: Integrating the interior and boundary comparisons and applying spherical stability bounds the positive mass term by three cutoff error terms.
- **claim-exclusion-five-six** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cut-exclusion)): The first dimension admitting a nonflat one-homogeneous global minimizer cannot be $5$ or $6$.
  - Explanation: The cutoff errors vanish in the limit while the positive mass stays strictly positive, a contradiction; so the first dimension with a nonflat minimizing cone is neither of the two excluded dimensions.

        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-red-original-link",
        "exact_quote": "Every point of $\\partial\\{u>0\\}\\setminus\\{0\\}$ is regular. Put $m_o=d-1$ and $g_o=u|_{S^{m_o}}$. The set $\\{g_o>0\\}$ is nonempty and proper, has finitely many connected components, and their closures are pairwise disjoint compact smooth manifolds with boundary. On each such component $\\Omega_o$, the restriction of $g_o$ is smooth up to the boundary from within $\\Omega_o$ and satisfies $$\\label{red:link-equations}\n \\Delta_{S^{m_o}}g_o=-m_o g_o\\quad\\hbox{in }\\Omega_o,\n \\qquad g_o=0,\\quad \\nabla_{S^{m_o}}g_o=N_o\n       \\quad\\hbox{on }\\partial\\Omega_o,$$ where $N_o$ is the inward spherical unit normal. In particular, $\\partial\\Omega_o\\ne\\varnothing$.",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-red-nonflat-component",
        "exact_quote": "There is a component $\\Omega_o$ in Proposition <a href=\"#red:original-link\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"red:original-link\">6</a> such that $$\\label{red:original-hessian}\n T_o:=\\nabla^2_{S^{m_o}}g_o+g_o\\mathop{\\mathrm{Id}}\\not\\equiv0\n \\quad\\hbox{on }\\Omega_o.$$ Here $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Id}}$ denotes the spherical metric, viewed as a symmetric two-tensor.",

        "anchor": "stmt-red-suspension",
        "exact_quote": "If $u\\geq0$ is a global minimizer in $\\mathbb R^k$, then $\\widetilde u(x,s)=u(x)$ is a global minimizer in $\\mathbb R^{k+1}$. If $u$ is the cone in <a href=\"#red:first-dimension\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"red:first-dimension\">[red:first-dimension]</a> with $d=5$, the singular free-boundary set of $\\widetilde u$ is exactly $\\{0\\}\\times\\mathbb R$. For the component chosen in Lemma <a href=\"#red:nonflat-component\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"red:nonflat-component\">7</a>, the corresponding positive component on $S^5$ is $$\\label{red:suspension-coordinates}\n \\widetilde\\Omega\n =\\bigl\\{\\Phi(t,y):=(\\sin t\\,y,\\cos t):\n              0<t<\\pi,\\ y\\in\\Omega_o\\bigr\\},\n \\qquad\n \\widetilde g(\\Phi(t,y))=\\sin t\\,g_o(y).$$ Its boundary consists of the smooth side $\\Phi((0,\\pi)\\times\\partial\\Omega_o)$ and the two singular tips $p_+=(0,1)$ and $p_-=(0,-1)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-367.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-geo-interior-identities",
        "exact_quote": "The tensor $T$ is symmetric and trace-free, and $B$ is fully symmetric and trace-free in every pair of slots. In particular $\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}T=0$. The following identities hold: $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\nabla_lB_{ijk}-\\nabla_iB_{ljk}\n &=\\delta_{lj}T_{ik}+\\delta_{lk}T_{ij}\n   -\\delta_{ij}T_{lk}-\\delta_{ik}T_{lj},\n \\label{geo:commutator}\\\\\n \\Delta T&=mT,\n &\\Delta w^2&=2m w^2+2|B|^2,\n \\label{geo:bochner}\\\\\n \\nabla_l p_i&=T_{li}-g\\delta_{li},\n &\\nabla_l g&=p_l,\n \\label{geo:first-jets}\\\\\n \\nabla s^2&=2Tp,\n &\\Delta s^2&=2w^2.\n \\label{geo:s-identities}\n\\end{aligned}$$ On the open set $\\{w>0\\}$ one also has $$\\label{geo:w-gradient}\n \\nabla_l w=w^{-1}T_{jk}B_{ljk},\\qquad |\\nabla w|\\le |B|.$$ The inequality $|\\nabla(w^2)|^2\\le4w^2|B|^2$ holds everywhere, including at $w=0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-367.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-geo-interior-identities",
          "exact_quote": "The tensor $T$ is symmetric and trace-free, and $B$ is fully symmetric and trace-free in every pair of slots. In particular $\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}T=0$. The following identities hold: $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\nabla_lB_{ijk}-\\nabla_iB_{ljk}\n &=\\delta_{lj}T_{ik}+\\delta_{lk}T_{ij}\n   -\\delta_{ij}T_{lk}-\\delta_{ik}T_{lj},\n \\label{geo:commutator}\\\\\n \\Delta T&=mT,\n &\\Delta w^2&=2m w^2+2|B|^2,\n \\label{geo:bochner}\\\\\n \\nabla_l p_i&=T_{li}-g\\delta_{li},\n &\\nabla_l g&=p_l,\n \\label{geo:first-jets}\\\\\n \\nabla s^2&=2Tp,\n &\\Delta s^2&=2w^2.\n \\label{geo:s-identities}\n\\end{aligned}$$ On the open set $\\{w>0\\}$ one also has $$\\label{geo:w-gradient}\n \\nabla_l w=w^{-1}T_{jk}B_{ljk},\\qquad |\\nabla w|\\le |B|.$$ The inequality $|\\nabla(w^2)|^2\\le4w^2|B|^2$ holds everywhere, including at $w=0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-367.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "The tensor $T$ is symmetric and trace-free, and $B$ is fully symmetric and trace-free in every pair of slots. In particular $\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}T=0$.       "explanation": "The spherical Hessian tensor T is symmetric, trace-free and divergence-free, and its derivative B is fully symmetric. These give the commutator, Laplacian and Bochner identities used throughout.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-geo-gradient-bound",
        "exact_quote": "On $\\Omega_o$ one has $s_o^2<1$. At every boundary point, put $$\\label{geo:H-definition}\n H_o=-T_o(N_o,N_o).$$ Then $$\\label{geo:strict-boundary-sign}\n H_o>0,\\qquad T_op_o=-H_op_o,\\qquad w_o>0\n \\quad\\hbox{on }\\partial\\Omega_o.$$ Consequently $w_o$ has a positive minimum on $\\partial\\Omega_o$, and its zero set is a compact subset of $\\Omega_o$.",
        "source_id": "PM-367.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-geo-gradient-bound",
          "exact_quote": "On $\\Omega_o$ one has $s_o^2<1$.           "source_id": "PM-367.1"

      "explanation": "On the original component the combined function-and-gradient quantity stays strictly below its boundary value. The boundary curvature is positive and the Hessian norm is positive on the boundary, so its zero set is compact in the interior.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-geo-boundary-jets",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $g=0$, $p=N$, and $T(N,N)=-H<0$ on a smooth boundary portion, where $N$ is inward. Let Greek indices be tangent to this boundary and put $$\\mathfrak h_{\\alpha\\beta}\n =\\langle\\nabla_{\\alpha}N,e_\\beta\\rangle.$$ Then $T_{N\\alpha}=0$, $T_{\\alpha\\beta}=\\mathfrak h_{\\alpha\\beta}$, and $H=\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}\\mathfrak h$; in particular $H$ is a sum of principal curvatures with the specified convention, without division by $m-1$. If $\\nabla^{\\partial}$ is the boundary connection, all components of $B$ are described, up to permutation of slots, by $$\\begin{aligned}\n B_{\\alpha\\beta\\gamma}\n   &=(\\nabla^{\\partial}_{\\alpha}\\mathfrak h)_{\\beta\\gamma},\n &B_{NN\\alpha}&=-\\partial_\\alpha H,\n \\label{geo:even-boundary-jets}\\\\\n B_{N\\alpha\\beta}\n   &=T_{NN}T_{\\alpha\\beta}-(T^2)_{\\alpha\\beta},\n &B_{NNN}&=w^2.\n \\label{geo:odd-boundary-jets}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The components with an odd number of normal slots are thus determined by the pointwise tensor $T$; the other components involve tangential derivatives of curvature. The tensor $V$ below encodes this odd-normal part after normalization. Here $w>0$. Define the normalized tensors and eigenvalue $$A=T/w,\\qquad \\lambda=T_{NN}/w=-H/w,\n \\qquad S_0=\\lambda A-A^2,$$ and the symmetric tensor $$\\label{geo:boundary-V}\n V_{ijk}=p_i(S_0)_{jk}+p_j(S_0)_{ki}+p_k(S_0)_{ij}\n           +p_ip_jp_k.$$ Then $|p|=1$, $Ap=\\lambda p$, $\\lambda<0$, $S_0p=0$, $\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}S_0=-1$, and $V$ is trace-free. The tensors $B/w^2$ and $V$ agree in every component with an odd number of normal slots. Moreover, $$\\label{geo:normal-w}\n V_{Njk}=(S_0+p\\otimes p)_{jk},\\qquad\n \\partial_N w=w^2\\bigl(2\\lambda-\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}A^3\\bigr).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-367.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-geo-boundary-jets",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose $g=0$, $p=N$, and $T(N,N)=-H<0$ on a smooth boundary portion, where $N$ is inward. Let Greek indices be tangent to this boundary and put $$\\mathfrak h_{\\alpha\\beta}\n =\\langle\\nabla_{\\alpha}N,e_\\beta\\rangle.$$ Then $T_{N\\alpha}=0$, $T_{\\alpha\\beta}=\\mathfrak h_{\\alpha\\beta}$, and $H=\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}\\mathfrak h$; in particular $H$ is a sum of principal curvatures with the specified convention, without division by $m-1$. If $\\nabla^{\\partial}$ is the boundary connection, all components of $B$ are described, up to permutation of slots, by $$\\begin{aligned}\n B_{\\alpha\\beta\\gamma}\n   &=(\\nabla^{\\partial}_{\\alpha}\\mathfrak h)_{\\beta\\gamma},\n &B_{NN\\alpha}&=-\\partial_\\alpha H,\n \\label{geo:even-boundary-jets}\\\\\n B_{N\\alpha\\beta}\n   &=T_{NN}T_{\\alpha\\beta}-(T^2)_{\\alpha\\beta},\n &B_{NNN}&=w^2.\n \\label{geo:odd-boundary-jets}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The components with an odd number of normal slots are thus determined by the pointwise tensor $T$; the other components involve tangential derivatives of curvature. The tensor $V$ below encodes this odd-normal part after normalization. Here $w>0$. Define the normalized tensors and eigenvalue $$A=T/w,\\qquad \\lambda=T_{NN}/w=-H/w,\n \\qquad S_0=\\lambda A-A^2,$$ and the symmetric tensor $$\\label{geo:boundary-V}\n V_{ijk}=p_i(S_0)_{jk}+p_j(S_0)_{ki}+p_k(S_0)_{ij}\n           +p_ip_jp_k.$$ Then $|p|=1$, $Ap=\\lambda p$, $\\lambda<0$, $S_0p=0$, $\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}S_0=-1$, and $V$ is trace-free. The tensors $B/w^2$ and $V$ agree in every component with an odd number of normal slots. Moreover, $$\\label{geo:normal-w}\n V_{Njk}=(S_0+p\\otimes p)_{jk},\\qquad\n \\partial_N w=w^2\\bigl(2\\lambda-\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}A^3\\bigr).$$",

      "exact_statement": "Suppose $g=0$, $p=N$, and $T(N,N)=-H<0$ on a smooth boundary portion, where $N$ is inward. Let Greek indices be tangent to this boundary and put $$\\mathfrak h_{\\alpha\\beta}\n =\\langle\\nabla_{\\alpha}N,e_\\beta\\rangle.$$ Then $T_{N\\alpha}=0$, $T_{\\alpha\\beta}=\\mathfrak h_{\\alpha\\beta}$, and $H=\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}\\mathfrak h$; in particular $H$ is a sum of principal curvatures with the specified convention, without division by $m-1$. If $\\nabla^{\\partial}$ is the boundary connection, all components of $B$ are described, up to permutation of slots, by $$\\begin{aligned}\n B_{\\alpha\\beta\\gamma}\n   &=(\\nabla^{\\partial}_{\\alpha}\\mathfrak h)_{\\beta\\gamma},\n &B_{NN\\alpha}&=-\\partial_\\alpha H,\n \\label{geo:even-boundary-jets}\\\\\n B_{N\\alpha\\beta}\n   &=T_{NN}T_{\\alpha\\beta}-(T^2)_{\\alpha\\beta},\n &B_{NNN}&=w^2.\n \\label{geo:odd-boundary-jets}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The components with an odd number of normal slots are thus determined by the pointwise tensor $T$; the other components involve tangential derivatives of curvature. The tensor $V$ below encodes this odd-normal part after normalization. Here $w>0$. Define the normalized tensors and eigenvalue $$A=T/w,\\qquad \\lambda=T_{NN}/w=-H/w,\n \\qquad S_0=\\lambda A-A^2,$$ and the symmetric tensor $$\\label{geo:boundary-V}\n V_{ijk}=p_i(S_0)_{jk}+p_j(S_0)_{ki}+p_k(S_0)_{ij}\n           +p_ip_jp_k.$$ Then $|p|=1$, $Ap=\\lambda p$, $\\lambda<0$, $S_0p=0$, $\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}S_0=-1$, and $V$ is trace-free. The tensors $B/w^2$ and $V$ agree in every component with an odd number of normal slots. Moreover, $$\\label{geo:normal-w}\n V_{Njk}=(S_0+p\\otimes p)_{jk},\\qquad\n \\partial_N w=w^2\\bigl(2\\lambda-\\mathop{\\mathrm{tr}}A^3\\bigr).$$",
      "explanation": "On the free boundary the components of the third-order tensor with an odd number of normal slots are determined by the Hessian tensor. The normalized tensor V encodes them and gives the normal derivative of w.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-geo-boundary-jets",

        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-geo-suspension-data",
        "exact_quote": "If $m_o=4$, write $\\rho=\\sin t$, $c=\\cos t$ and parameterize the regular part of the suspended component by $$(t,y)\\longmapsto (\\rho y,c),\\qquad\n 0<t<\\pi,\\quad y\\in\\Omega_o.$$ Its metric is $dt^2+\\rho^2 ds_{S^4}^2$, and $\\widetilde g=\\rho g_o$. With $E_0=\\partial_t$ and $E_\\alpha=\\rho^{-1}e_\\alpha$ for a lifted orthonormal frame on $S^4$, the data satisfy $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\widetilde p_0&=c g_o,\n &\\widetilde p_\\alpha&=(p_o)_\\alpha,\n &\\widetilde s^2&=s_o^2,\n \\label{geo:suspended-first-jets}\\\\\n \\widetilde T_{00}&=0,\n &\\widetilde T_{0\\alpha}&=0,\n &\\widetilde T_{\\alpha\\beta}&=\\rho^{-1}(T_o)_{\\alpha\\beta}.\n \\label{geo:suspended-T}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The only possibly nonzero components of $\\widetilde B$ are $$\\label{geo:suspended-B}\n \\widetilde B_{\\alpha\\beta\\gamma}\n   =\\rho^{-2}(B_o)_{\\alpha\\beta\\gamma},\\qquad\n \\widetilde B_{0\\alpha\\beta}\n =\\widetilde B_{\\alpha0\\beta}\n =\\widetilde B_{\\alpha\\beta0}\n   =-c\\rho^{-2}(T_o)_{\\alpha\\beta}.$$ In particular, $$\\label{geo:suspended-norms}\n \\widetilde w=\\rho^{-1}w_o,\\qquad\n |\\widetilde B|^2\n =\\rho^{-4}\\bigl(|B_o|^2+3c^2w_o^2\\bigr),\\qquad\n \\widetilde H=\\rho^{-1}H_o\n \\quad\\hbox{on the regular side.}$$ The volume and side-boundary measures are $$\\label{geo:suspended-measures}\n dV=\\rho^4\\,dt\\,dV_o,\\qquad\n d\\sigma=\\rho^3\\,dt\\,d\\sigma_o.$$ Every local identity of Proposition <a href=\"#geo:interior-identities\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"geo:interior-identities\">9</a> holds with $m=5$, and Proposition <a href=\"#geo:boundary-jets\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"geo:boundary-jets\">11</a> holds on the regular side. Also $\\widetilde s^2<1$ inside and $\\widetilde H,\\widetilde w>0$ on that side.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-geo-euclidean-stability",
        "exact_quote": "Let $D$ be the selected positivity component of a global minimizing cone in $\\mathbb R^{m+1}$. Let $\\phi$ be any real smooth function up to the regular boundary of $D$, with compact support in that regular closure away from the vertex and from any other singular points. Then $$\\label{geo:euclidean-inequality}\n \\int_D |\\nabla\\phi|^2\\,dx\n \\ge \\int_{\\partial D}H_E\\phi^2\\,d\\sigma_E,\n \\qquad H_E=-D^2u(N_E,N_E),$$ where $N_E$ is the inward Euclidean unit normal. No restriction is imposed on the boundary trace or normal derivative of $\\phi$.",
        "source_id": "PM-367.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-geo-euclidean-stability",
          "exact_quote": "Let $D$ be the selected positivity component of a global minimizing cone in $\\mathbb R^{m+1}$. Then $$\\label{geo:euclidean-inequality}\n \\int_D |\\nabla\\phi|^2\\,dx\n \\ge \\int_{\\partial D}H_E\\phi^2\\,d\\sigma_E,\n \\qquad H_E=-D^2u(N_E,N_E),$$ where $N_E$ is the inward Euclidean unit normal. No restriction is imposed on the boundary trace or normal derivative of $\\phi$.",

        }

Then $$\\label{geo:euclidean-inequality}\n \\int_D |\\nabla\\phi|^2\\,dx\n \\ge \\int_{\\partial D}H_E\\phi^2\\,d\\sigma_E,\n \\qquad H_E=-D^2u(N_E,N_E),$$ where $N_E$ is the inward Euclidean unit normal.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-geo-spherical-stability",
        "exact_quote": "For every real function $v$ smooth up to the regular boundary of the working component $\\Omega\\subset S^5$, with compact support in its closure away from the tips if present, $$\\label{geo:spherical-inequality}\n \\int_{\\partial\\Omega}H v^2\\,d\\sigma\n \\le \\int_\\Omega\\bigl(|\\nabla v|^2+4v^2\\bigr)\\,dV.$$ The trace of $v$ on the regular boundary is arbitrary.",
        "source_id": "PM-367.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-geo-spherical-stability",
          "exact_quote": "For every real function $v$ smooth up to the regular boundary of the working component $\\Omega\\subset S^5$, with compact support in its closure away from the tips if present, $$\\label{geo:spherical-inequality}\n \\int_{\\partial\\Omega}H v^2\\,d\\sigma\n \\le \\int_\\Omega\\bigl(|\\nabla v|^2+4v^2\\bigr)\\,dV.$$ The trace of $v$ on the regular boundary is arbitrary.",
          "source_id": "PM-367.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "For every real function $v$ smooth up to the regular boundary of the working component $\\Omega\\subset S^5$, with compact support in its closure away from the tips if present, $$\\label{geo:spherical-inequality}\n \\int_{\\partial\\Omega}H v^2\\,d\\sigma\n \\le \\int_\\Omega\\bigl(|\\nabla v|^2+4v^2\\bigr)\\,dV.$$ The trace of $v$ on the regular boundary is arbitrary.",
      "explanation": "Separating the radial variable and using the sharp radial Hardy constant turns the Euclidean stability inequality into one on the spherical link, with an arbitrary boundary trace.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cert-universal",
        "exact_quote": "There are rational tensor polynomials $f,U_{jk},K_{lijk},M_l,P_l$ in $A,p,g,\\delta$, with the following properties. They are equivariant under orthogonal changes of coordinates, with $f$ invariant. Their coefficients are specified by Table I in Appendix <a href=\"#app:tables\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"app:tables\">9</a>, using the decoding in Section <a href=\"#cert:decode\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"cert:decode\">6.1</a>. Put $$C_{lijk}=K_{lijk}-K_{iljk},\\qquad\n G_{lijk}=\\delta_{li}U_{jk}+C_{lijk}.$$ For every choice of the data above with $\\chi\\ge0$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathcal I:={}&z^2\\left[5U_{jk}A_{jk}\n       +C_{lijk}(\\delta_{lj}A_{ik}+\\delta_{lk}A_{ij})-4f\\right]\\notag\\\\\n &+\\left[D_lG_{lijk}+(2a-1)q_lG_{lijk}\\right]X_{ijk}\\notag\\\\\n &+w\\left[D_lM_l+(2a+1)q_lM_l\\right]\n       +z\\left[D_lP_l+2aq_lP_l\\right]\\notag\\\\\n &+f|\\ell|^2-(D_lf+2aq_lf)\\ell_l\n \\ \\ge\\ \\epsilon_*(z^2+|\\ell|^2).\\label{cert:interior}\n\\end{aligned}$$ In particular, $f\\ge\\epsilon_*$ whenever $g^2+|p|^2\\le1$.*\n\n\n*For every symmetric trace-free unit matrix $A$ and vector $p$ satisfying $$g=0,\\qquad |p|=1,\\qquad Ap=\\lambda p,\\qquad\\lambda<0,$$ define $$S_0=\\lambda A-A^2,\\qquad\n V_{ijk}=p_i(S_0)_{jk}+p_j(S_0)_{ki}+p_k(S_0)_{ij}+p_ip_jp_k.$$ Then $$\\label{cert:boundary}\n \\mathcal S:=-\\lambda f+U_{jk}(S_0+p\\otimes p)_{jk}\n                 +C_{lijk}p_lV_{ijk}+p_lM_l\n \\ \\ge\\ \\delta_*.$$ Moreover, $U$ and $K$ are even in $p$, and $P=0$ when $g=0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-367.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cert-universal",
          "exact_quote": "There are rational tensor polynomials $f,U_{jk},K_{lijk},M_l,P_l$ in $A,p,g,\\delta$, with the following properties. They are equivariant under orthogonal changes of coordinates, with $f$ invariant. Their coefficients are specified by Table I in Appendix <a href=\"#app:tables\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"app:tables\">9</a>, using the decoding in Section <a href=\"#cert:decode\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"cert:decode\">6.1</a>. Put $$C_{lijk}=K_{lijk}-K_{iljk},\\qquad\n G_{lijk}=\\delta_{li}U_{jk}+C_{lijk}.$$ For every choice of the data above with $\\chi\\ge0$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathcal I:={}&z^2\\left[5U_{jk}A_{jk}\n       +C_{lijk}(\\delta_{lj}A_{ik}+\\delta_{lk}A_{ij})-4f\\right]\\notag\\\\\n &+\\left[D_lG_{lijk}+(2a-1)q_lG_{lijk}\\right]X_{ijk}\\notag\\\\\n &+w\\left[D_lM_l+(2a+1)q_lM_l\\right]\n       +z\\left[D_lP_l+2aq_lP_l\\right]\\notag\\\\\n &+f|\\ell|^2-(D_lf+2aq_lf)\\ell_l\n \\ \\ge\\ \\epsilon_*(z^2+|\\ell|^2).\\label{cert:interior}\n\\end{aligned}$$ In particular, $f\\ge\\epsilon_*$ whenever $g^2+|p|^2\\le1$.*\n\n\n*For every symmetric trace-free unit matrix $A$ and vector $p$ satisfying $$g=0,\\qquad |p|=1,\\qquad Ap=\\lambda p,\\qquad\\lambda<0,$$ define $$S_0=\\lambda A-A^2,\\qquad\n V_{ijk}=p_i(S_0)_{jk}+p_j(S_0)_{ki}+p_k(S_0)_{ij}+p_ip_jp_k.$$ Then $$\\label{cert:boundary}\n \\mathcal S:=-\\lambda f+U_{jk}(S_0+p\\otimes p)_{jk}\n                 +C_{lijk}p_lV_{ijk}+p_lM_l\n \\ \\ge\\ \\delta_*.$$ Moreover, $U$ and $K$ are even in $p$, and $P=0$ when $g=0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-367.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "There are rational tensor polynomials $f,U_{jk},K_{lijk},M_l,P_l$ in $A,p,g,\\delta$, with the following properties. They are equivariant under orthogonal changes of coordinates, with $f$ invariant. Their coefficients are specified by Table I in Appendix <a href=\"#app:tables\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"app:tables\">9</a>, using the decoding in Section <a href=\"#cert:decode\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"cert:decode\">6.1</a>. Put $$C_{lijk}=K_{lijk}-K_{iljk},\\qquad\n G_{lijk}=\\delta_{li}U_{jk}+C_{lijk}.$$ For every choice of the data above with $\\chi\\ge0$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathcal I:={}&z^2\\left[5U_{jk}A_{jk}\n       +C_{lijk}(\\delta_{lj}A_{ik}+\\delta_{lk}A_{ij})-4f\\right]\\notag\\\\\n &+\\left[D_lG_{lijk}+(2a-1)q_lG_{lijk}\\right]X_{ijk}\\notag\\\\\n &+w\\left[D_lM_l+(2a+1)q_lM_l\\right]\n       +z\\left[D_lP_l+2aq_lP_l\\right]\\notag\\\\\n &+f|\\ell|^2-(D_lf+2aq_lf)\\ell_l\n \\ \\ge\\ \\epsilon_*(z^2+|\\ell|^2).\\label{cert:interior}\n\\end{aligned}$$ In particular, $f\\ge\\epsilon_*$ whenever $g^2+|p|^2\\le1$.*\n\n\n*For every symmetric trace-free unit matrix $A$ and vector $p$ satisfying $$g=0,\\qquad |p|=1,\\qquad Ap=\\lambda p,\\qquad\\lambda<0,$$ define $$S_0=\\lambda A-A^2,\\qquad\n V_{ijk}=p_i(S_0)_{jk}+p_j(S_0)_{ki}+p_k(S_0)_{ij}+p_ip_jp_k.$$ Then $$\\label{cert:boundary}\n \\mathcal S:=-\\lambda f+U_{jk}(S_0+p\\otimes p)_{jk}\n                 +C_{lijk}p_lV_{ijk}+p_lM_l\n \\ \\ge\\ \\delta_*.$$ Moreover, $U$ and $K$ are even in $p$, and $P=0$ when $g=0$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-flux-normalized-differentiation",
        "exact_quote": "At the data <a href=\"#flux:normalized-data\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"flux:normalized-data\">[flux:normalized-data]</a>, the derivation $D_l$ of <a href=\"#cert:D\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cert:D\">[cert:D]</a>, evaluated at $z=1$, equals covariant differentiation on every coefficient polynomial in $A,p,g,\\delta$. In particular, $$\\label{flux:first-derivatives}\n \\nabla_lw=wq_l,\\qquad\n \\nabla_lA_{ij}=X_{lij}-q_lA_{ij},\\qquad\n \\nabla_lp_i=wA_{li}-g\\delta_{li},\\qquad\n \\nabla_lg=p_l.$$ Furthermore, $$\\label{flux:fourth-elimination}\n \\frac1wG_{lijk}\\nabla_lB_{ijk}\n =5U_{jk}A_{jk}\n   +C_{lijk}(\\delta_{lj}A_{ik}+\\delta_{lk}A_{ij}).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-367.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-flux-normalized-differentiation",
          "exact_quote": "At the data <a href=\"#flux:normalized-data\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"flux:normalized-data\">[flux:normalized-data]</a>, the derivation $D_l$ of <a href=\"#cert:D\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cert:D\">[cert:D]</a>, evaluated at $z=1$, equals covariant differentiation on every coefficient polynomial in $A,p,g,\\delta$. In particular, $$\\label{flux:first-derivatives}\n \\nabla_lw=wq_l,\\qquad\n \\nabla_lA_{ij}=X_{lij}-q_lA_{ij},\\qquad\n \\nabla_lp_i=wA_{li}-g\\delta_{li},\\qquad\n \\nabla_lg=p_l.$$ Furthermore, $$\\label{flux:fourth-elimination}\n \\frac1wG_{lijk}\\nabla_lB_{ijk}\n =5U_{jk}A_{jk}\n   +C_{lijk}(\\delta_{lj}A_{ik}+\\delta_{lk}A_{ij}).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-367.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "At the data <a href=\"#flux:normalized-data\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"flux:normalized-data\">[flux:normalized-data]</a>, the derivation $D_l$ of <a href=\"#cert:D\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cert:D\">[cert:D]</a>, evaluated at $z=1$, equals covariant differentiation on every coefficient polynomial in $A,p,g,\\delta$. In particular, $$\\label{flux:first-derivatives}\n \\nabla_lw=wq_l,\\qquad\n \\nabla_lA_{ij}=X_{lij}-q_lA_{ij},\\qquad\n \\nabla_lp_i=wA_{li}-g\\delta_{li},\\qquad\n \\nabla_lg=p_l.$$ Furthermore, $$\\label{flux:fourth-elimination}\n \\frac1wG_{lijk}\\nabla_lB_{ijk}\n =5U_{jk}A_{jk}\n   +C_{lijk}(\\delta_{lj}A_{ik}+\\delta_{lk}A_{ij}).$$",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **global minimizer** ([source](#eq:energy)): A nonnegative $u\in H^1_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb R^d)$ is a *global minimizer* if $J(u;B)\leq J(v;B)$ for every ball $B$ and every nonnegative $v\in H^1(B)$ with $v-u\in H^1_0(B)$.
- **one-homogeneous function** ([source](#eq:energy)): A function is *one-homogeneous* if $u(rx)=r u(x)$ for every $r>0$, almost everywhere in $x$.
- **flat solutions** ([source](#eq:energy)): The nonzero flat solutions are $$u(x)=(x\cdot e)_+,\qquad \|e\|=1.$$
- **critical dimension d_*** ([source](#eq:energy)): Let $d_*$ be the least dimension containing a nonzero, nonflat, one-homogeneous global minimizer.
- **curvature convention** ([source](#pm-h-4)): The curvature $H=-T(N,N)$ used below is not divided by the dimension of the boundary.
- **suspension squared quantity notation** ([source](#geo:ambient-hessian)): The notation $s^2$ denotes the displayed smooth function, including at points where it vanishes.
- **trace-free tensor** ([source](#sec:certificate)): A symmetric tensor is called trace-free if contraction of any two slots is zero.
- **space of symmetric trace-free tensors** ([source](#sec:certificate)): We write $\mathop{\mathrm{STF}}^r(\mathbb R^5)$ for the space of symmetric trace-free tensors of rank $r$.
- **norm parameter theta** ([source](#cert:bounds)): Put $\theta=4/5$.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-regularity** [claim](#stmt-red-regularity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-red-regularity): Cite constant-coefficient one-phase regularity and compactness theory, check the viscosity condition, then apply higher-order and boundary regularity. -> Lipschitz representative, blowups as minimizing cones, smooth free boundary at flat blowups
- **step-translation** [claim](#stmt-red-translation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-red-translation): Use homogeneity to compare two rescalings and pass to the limit along the same sequence. -> translation invariance of off-vertex blowups
- **step-factor-minimality** [claim](#stmt-red-factor-minimality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-red-factor-minimality): Argue by contradiction with a cylindrical competitor and a long cutoff in the extra variable. -> factor is a global minimizer
- **step-original-link** [claim](#stmt-red-original-link) / [proof](#proof-stmt-red-original-link): Reduce off-vertex blowups to lower-dimensional flat minimizers, then use polar coordinates and regular charts on the sphere. -> smooth spherical link with finitely many components
- **step-nonflat-component** [claim](#stmt-red-nonflat-component) / [proof](#proof-stmt-red-nonflat-component): If the Hessian tensor vanished on all components, each would be a half-space, contradicting disjoint closures. -> component with nonzero tensor
- **step-suspension** [claim](#stmt-red-suspension) / [proof](#proof-stmt-red-suspension): Slice the ball, use Fubini and slice-wise minimality, then identify the singular axis and the suspended component. -> suspended global minimizer with two tips
- **step-interior-identities** [claim](#stmt-geo-interior-identities) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-interior-identities): Codazzi property, curvature commutation and product rule. -> symmetry, Bochner and gradient identities
- **step-gradient-bound** [claim](#stmt-geo-gradient-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-gradient-bound): Subharmonicity, strong maximum principle and Hopf lemma. -> strict gradient bound and positive boundary curvature
- **step-boundary-jets** [claim](#stmt-geo-boundary-jets) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-boundary-jets): Differentiate boundary conditions tangentially and use the trace-free property of the derivative tensor. -> boundary jet formulas and normalized tensor V
- **step-suspension-data** [claim](#stmt-geo-suspension-data) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-suspension-data): Compute the warped-product connection and differentiate the suspended function. -> explicit data on the suspended working component
- **step-euclidean-stability** [claim](#stmt-geo-euclidean-stability) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-euclidean-stability): Build an admissible competitor by extending the positive side, expand the energy to second order using a boundary layer, and use minimality. -> second-variation inequality
- **step-spherical-stability** [claim](#stmt-geo-spherical-stability) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-spherical-stability): Separate variables and take the radial Hardy limit. -> spherical stability inequality
- **step-positive-projections** [claim](#stmt-cert-positive-projections) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cert-positive-projections): Identify each operator as a scaled orthogonal projection. -> nonnegativity of Gram contributions
- **step-graph-closure** [claim](#stmt-cert-closure) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cert-closure): Induction over the contraction and derivative operations on graphs. -> finite sum of degree-two scalar graphs
- **step-tensor-bounds** [claim](#stmt-cert-tensor-bounds) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cert-tensor-bounds): Eigenvalue estimates, Lagrange multipliers and an explicit projection computation. -> operator, trace and contraction bounds
- **step-graph-bound** [claim](#stmt-cert-graph-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cert-graph-bound): Classify connected components of a graph and apply tensor Cauchy-Schwarz with the tensor bounds. -> rational bound for each graph
- **step-universal-certificate** [claim](#stmt-cert-universal) / [proof](#sec:certificate-proof): Exact finite rational arithmetic with rounded graph bounds, weighted Young inequalities for the interior, and eleven boundary coefficients for the boundary. -> interior and boundary inequalities with positive margins
- **step-normalized-differentiation** [claim](#stmt-flux-normalized-differentiation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-flux-normalized-differentiation): Match the derivation rules with covariant derivatives and use the Laplacian and skew contractions. -> elimination of fourth derivatives
- **step-interior-comparison** [claim](#stmt-flux-interior-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-flux-interior-comparison): Compute the divergence of Z, complete the square in the free vector, and use the interior inequality. -> divergence inequality
- **step-boundary-parity** [claim](#stmt-flux-boundary-parity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-flux-boundary-parity): Reflection in the normal hyperplane and parity of the coefficient tensors. -> boundary contraction identity
- **step-boundary-comparison** [claim](#stmt-flux-boundary-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-flux-boundary-comparison): Combine the parity identity with the boundary polynomial inequality. -> signed boundary flux bound
- **step-field-bounds** [claim](#stmt-flux-field-bounds) / [proof](#proof-stmt-flux-field-bounds): Compactness of the coefficient domain and Cauchy-Schwarz. -> uniform bounds on h, its gradient and Z
- **step-weighted-bochner** [claim](#stmt-cut-weighted-bochner) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cut-weighted-bochner): Regularize the squared norm, integrate by parts against a cutoff, absorb the error and use monotone convergence. -> integrable dominating function
- **step-integrated-comparison** [claim](#stmt-cut-integrated-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cut-integrated-comparison): Integrate the interior inequality, apply the divergence theorem with the boundary inequality, then use spherical stability. -> mass bounded by three cutoff errors
- **step-exclusion** [claim](#stmt-cut-exclusion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cut-exclusion): Dominated convergence sends the errors to zero while the mass tends to a positive limit. -> contradiction in the two excluded dimensions
- **step-main-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cut-exclusion): Combine the exclusion with the known lower and upper bounds from the literature. -> critical dimension equals seven
- **step-singular-set** [claim](#stmt-cor-singular-set) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cut-exclusion-2): Apply dimension reduction with the critical dimension, then use products of the known cone with Euclidean space for sharpness. -> sharp singular-set bound

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-boundary-margin-arithmetic**: The boundary margin is the quarter minus the sum of the absolute values of the eleven integer coefficients divided by the square of the scaling denominator. With the printed sum this reproduces the stated boundary margin as an exact rational number.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-arithmetic-as-analytic-proof**: Treat a passing run of this finite arithmetic check as a proof of the analytic stability argument or of the critical-dimension theorem. -> The check only re-adds eleven integers and recomputes one rational margin. It says nothing about the correspondence between the formulas and the printed coefficients, nor about the geometric argument.
- **anti-flatness-in-seven**: Reuse the verified margin as evidence that nonflat minimizing cones are excluded in dimension seven. -> The certificate is used only to exclude the first nonflat dimension among five and six; in dimension seven a nonflat minimizer exists.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `algorithmic`. The boundary margin of the certificate is computed by a finite exact procedure: sum the absolute values of the eleven integer coefficients and subtract the result, scaled, from one quarter. The procedure is checked against the stated rational value. The analytic parts of the paper are not finite constructions and are not covered.

```python
# INPUTS: whitespace separated integers (the coefficient numerators), read from stdin.
# PRECONDITIONS: at least one integer; scaling denominator squared is fixed at the value used in the source.
# OUTPUTS: one line: sum of absolute values, then the exact rational one quarter minus sum over the fixed denominator.
# TIME: linear in the number of integers.
# SPACE: constant beyond the input list.
# ARITHMETIC: exact integers and exact rationals only, no floating point.
# FAILURE: non-integer token raises ValueError and the run exits with an error.
import sys
from fractions import Fraction

vals = [int(t) for t in sys.stdin.read().split()]
s = sum(abs(v) for v in vals)
d = Fraction(1, 4) - Fraction(s, 62500000000)
print(s, d)
```

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves analytic regularity statements about minimizers of a continuum energy and gives no program, interface or data format. Software can use the result only as a background guarantee, so nothing is constructed. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No simulation scheme or discretisation is given. The statements concern ideal minimizers of the one-phase energy, not time-stepping or real-time interface models. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper ships no tool contract. Its only finite computation is an exact arithmetic check of a tensor certificate, and the paper itself says that check covers finite arithmetic only and does not replace mathematical review. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The main results are existence and classification theorems proved by contradiction with a stability argument. They give no procedure that outputs an object from an input, so there is no algorithm to extract. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No representation or indexing structure is proposed. The spherical tensors and their coefficient tables serve only as proof data for an inequality. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper contains no hardware-oriented computation or dataflow. The finite certificate is a one-off exact verification, not a kernel to accelerate. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No cryptographic primitive, hardness assumption or security property appears. Variational free-boundary regularity has no bearing on adversarial models. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The theory covers a deterministic energy with the volume coefficient fixed, and says nothing about learning, generalisation or trained models. Using it for learned interface models would exceed the stated hypotheses. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The energy is minimised over Sobolev functions in a continuum, and the paper offers no solver, convergence rate or relaxation for optimisation practice. Only the structure of minimizers is described. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no communication, concurrency or fault model anywhere in the paper. The one-phase minimization problem has no distributed content. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The natural home is pure analysis of free boundaries, where the result fixes the threshold between smooth and singular interfaces. It also yields a suspension principle for minimality. It is not an engineering construction, so no applicable domain is claimed. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-dimension-seven-nonflat** (edge_case): Flatness of nonzero one-homogeneous global minimizers holds only for dimensions one to six. In dimension seven a nonflat one exists, so the smoothness conclusions for free boundaries do not extend to dimension seven or above. Blocked use: ['Assuming a smooth interior free boundary for a minimizer in dimension seven or higher', 'Reusing the dimension five and six exclusion argument in dimension seven']
- **limit-full-sobolev-minimality** (hypothesis): All results use minimality against arbitrary nonnegative Sobolev competitors with the prescribed trace on every ball. Critical points, stationary solutions or minimality against a restricted competitor class are not covered. Blocked use: ['Applying the flatness or singular-set results to stationary points that are not minimizers', 'Applying them to solutions minimal only among homogeneous or otherwise restricted competitors']
- **limit-one-phase-functional** (hypothesis): The theory is for the one-phase Bernoulli energy of a nonnegative function with the indicator term of unit weight. Two-phase problems and variable volume coefficients are outside the stated hypotheses. Blocked use: ['Transferring the critical dimension to two-phase free-boundary problems', 'Transferring the tensor certificate to problems with a different volume term']
- **limit-nonconstructive-exclusion** (nonconstructive): The exclusion of dimensions five and six is a proof by contradiction. It decides nothing about a given cone and yields no procedure that outputs an object. Blocked use: ['Using the exclusion argument as a decision procedure for flatness of a given cone', 'Extracting an algorithm or complexity bound from the contradiction argument']
- **limit-finite-arithmetic-scope** (formal_scope): The accompanying exact checks cover only finite arithmetic. The correspondence between formulas and code and the analytic argument are left to mathematical review. Blocked use: ['Treating a passing arithmetic check as a proof of the critical-dimension theorem', 'Treating it as an independent validation of the geometric derivation']
- **limit-certificate-dimension-five** (hypothesis): The algebraic certificate is stated for tensor spaces over the five-dimensional Euclidean space, with a fixed exponent and fixed rational margins. It is tied to the six-dimensional cone with five-dimensional spherical link. Blocked use: ['Reusing the certificate polynomials or margins for links of other dimensions', 'Changing the exponent a without redoing the exact verification']
- **limit-hessian-zero-set** (edge_case): The normalized Hessian and its derivatives are undefined where the Hessian norm vanishes. The argument handles this only through compactness of the zero set in the interior and a weighted integrability estimate, and uses no regularity of the zero set. Blocked use: ['Assuming the zero set of the Hessian is empty, smooth or of controlled dimension', 'Differentiating the normalized fields across the zero set without cutoffs']
- **limit-suspension-tips** (edge_case): In the suspended case the working component has two singular tips where the spherical data are not smooth. Tests must vanish near the tips, and all estimates are made on the regular part only. Blocked use: ['Integrating by parts across the tips without a cutoff', 'Treating the suspended component as a smooth manifold with boundary']

### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 367 (`lean/docs/367.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [The critical dimension for one-phase Bernoulli minimizers](../../preprints/The-critical-dimension-for-one-phase-Bernoulli-minimizers-September-24-2026/The-critical-dimension-for-one-phase-Bernoulli-minimizers-September-24-2026.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The paper identifies seven as the critical dimension for nonflat one-homogeneous global minimizers of the one-phase Bernoulli problem. The linked formalization proves the existence side: in dimension seven there is a nonzero one-homogeneous global minimizer that is not a half-space solution.
>
> The flatness classification in dimensions at most six and the resulting regularity and singular-set bounds are outside this selected statement.

Comparator source `BernoulliNonflatInSeven.lean` (3466 bytes, sha256 `47d90c7719ce7b54acbc5262a488f8f5c610691206f978ef84a9fad2c0803447`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

noncomputable section

open MeasureTheory Set Filter
open scoped ENNReal Topology ContDiff

namespace Bernoulli
open scoped _root_.Bernoulli

abbrev Space (d : ℕ) := EuclideanSpace ℝ (Fin d)

/-- Compactly supported smooth real tests in the open set. -/
def Test {d : ℕ} (D : Set (Space d)) (φ : Space d → ℝ) : Prop :=
  ContDiff ℝ ∞ φ ∧ HasCompactSupport φ ∧ tsupport φ ⊆ D

/-- Distributional gradient, tested in every constant direction. -/
def WeakGradient {d : ℕ} (D : Set (Space d))
    (u : Space d → ℝ) (G : Space d → Space d) : Prop :=
  ∀ φ, Test D φ → ∀ h : Space d,
    (∫ x in D, u x * (fderiv ℝ φ x) h) =
      -(∫ x in D, inner ℝ (G x) h * φ x)

/-- H¹(D), with its weak gradient. -/
def H1 {d : ℕ} (D : Set (Space d))
    (u : Space d → ℝ) (G : Space d → Space d) : Prop :=
  MemLp u 2 (volume.restrict D) ∧
  MemLp G 2 (volume.restrict D) ∧ WeakGradient D u G

/-- H¹₀(D) is H¹-closure of C∞ compactly supported functions.
Both L² errors tend to zero. This does not restrict competitors to smooth ones. -/
def H10 {d : ℕ} (D : Set (Space d))
    (u : Space d → ℝ) (G : Space d → Space d) : Prop :=
  H1 D u G ∧ ∀ ε : ℝ, 0 < ε → ∃ φ : Space d → ℝ,
    Test D φ ∧
    eLpNorm (fun x => u x - φ x) 2 (volume.restrict D) < ENNReal.ofReal ε ∧
    eLpNorm (fun x => G x - gradient φ x) 2 (volume.restrict D) < ENNReal.ofReal ε

/-- Ballwise H¹_loc, on an open set (balls have compact closure inside D). -/
def H1Loc {d : ℕ} (D : Set (Space d))
    (u : Space d → ℝ) (G : Space d → Space d) : Prop :=
  ∀ c : Space d, ∀ r : ℝ, 0 < r → Metric.closedBall c r ⊆ D →
    H1 (Metric.ball c r) u G

/-- The exact volume-one Bernoulli energy, not just Dirichlet energy. -/
def energy {d : ℕ} (D : Set (Space d))
    (u : Space d → ℝ) (G : Space d → Space d) : ℝ :=
  ∫ x in D, ‖G x‖ ^ 2 + if 0 < u x then (1 : ℝ) else 0

/-- Full ballwise local comparison. Competitor and gradient are arbitrary
Sobolev representatives on the ball, with zero-trace difference. -/
def LocalMinimizer {d : ℕ} (D : Set (Space d)) (u : Space d → ℝ) : Prop :=
  IsOpen D ∧ ∃ G : Space d → Space d,
    H1Loc D u G ∧ (∀ᵐ x ∂volume.restrict D, 0 ≤ u x) ∧
    ∀ c : Space d, ∀ r : ℝ, 0 < r → Metric.closedBall c r ⊆ D →
      ∀ (v : Space d → ℝ) (V : Space d → Space d),
        H1 (Metric.ball c r) v V →
        (∀ᵐ x ∂volume.restrict (Metric.ball c r), 0 ≤ v x) →
        H10 (Metric.ball c r) (fun x => v x - u x) (fun x => V x - G x) →
        energy (Metric.ball c r) u G ≤ energy (Metric.ball c r) v V

/-- Nonnegative global minimizer, with all centers and all positive radii. -/
def GlobalMinimizer {d : ℕ} (u : Space d → ℝ) : Prop :=
  LocalMinimizer Set.univ u

def OneHomogeneous {d : ℕ} (u : Space d → ℝ) : Prop :=
  ∀ r : ℝ, 0 < r → ∀ᵐ x ∂volume, u (r • x) = r * u x

def Nonzero {d : ℕ} (u : Space d → ℝ) : Prop :=
  ¬ (u =ᵐ[volume] fun _ => 0)

/-- Flatness is a.e. equality of H¹ representatives. -/
def Flat {d : ℕ} (u : Space d → ℝ) : Prop :=
  ∃ e : Space d, ‖e‖ = 1 ∧ ∀ᵐ x ∂volume, u x = max (inner ℝ x e) 0

theorem nonflat_in_seven : ∃ u : Space 7 → ℝ,
    GlobalMinimizer u ∧ Nonzero u ∧ OneHomogeneous u ∧ ¬ Flat u := by
  sorry

end Bernoulli

end

end OAI
```

The linked formalization proves the existence side: in dimension seven there is a nonzero one-homogeneous global minimizer that is not a half-space solution.\n\nThe flatness classification in dimensions at most six and the resulting regularity and singular-set bounds are outside this selected statement."

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that seven is the critical dimension for the one-phase Bernoulli problem: every nonzero one-homogeneous global minimizer in dimensions at most six is flat, while a nonflat one-homogeneous global minimizer exists in dimension seven. It follows that the interior free boundary of a local minimizer is smooth in dimensions at most six. In dimension <i>n</i> ≥ 7, its singular set has Hausdorff dimension at most $`n-7`$, and this bound is sharp. In dimension seven, the singular set is locally finite.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

<a id="eq:energy"></a>
For a nonnegative function $v\in H^1(B)$, where $B\subset\mathbb R^d$ is a ball, the one-phase Bernoulli energy is $$\label{eq:energy}
  J(v;B)=\int_B\bigl(|\nabla v|^2+\mathbf 1_{\{v>0\}}\bigr)\,dx.$$ A nonnegative $u\in H^1_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb R^d)$ is a *global minimizer* if $J(u;B)\leq J(v;B)$ for every ball $B$ and every nonnegative $v\in H^1(B)$ with $v-u\in H^1_0(B)$. The competitors in these comparisons are arbitrary Sobolev functions with the prescribed trace. A function is *one-homogeneous* if $u(rx)=r u(x)$ for every $r>0$, almost everywhere in $x$. The nonzero flat solutions are $$u(x)=(x\cdot e)_+,\qquad |e|=1.$$ Let $d_*$ be the least dimension containing a nonzero, nonflat, one-homogeneous global minimizer.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *Every nonzero one-homogeneous global minimizer of <a href="#eq:energy" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:energy">[eq:energy]</a> in $\mathbb R^d$, $1\leq d\leq6$, is flat. There is a nonflat one-homogeneous global minimizer in $\mathbb R^7$. Consequently, $$d_*=7.$$*

</div>

A nonnegative $u\in H^1_{\mathrm{loc}}(D)$ is a local minimizer in an open set $D\subset\mathbb R^n$ if it satisfies the same comparison on every ball compactly contained in $D$. At an interior free-boundary point $x_0$, a *blowup* is a locally uniform limit of $u(x_0+rx)/r$ along radii tending to zero. A point is regular if one such limit is a flat solution, and singular otherwise. Write $\operatorname{Sing}(u)$ for the set of singular points in $D$.

<a id="stmt-cor-singular-set"></a>
<div id="cor:singular-set" class="corollary">

**Corollary 2** (Sharp singular-set bound). *Let $n\ge2$, let $D\subset\mathbb R^n$ be open, and let $u\in H^1_{\mathrm{loc}}(D)$ be a nonnegative local minimizer of the one-phase Bernoulli energy in $D$. Its interior free boundary is smooth outside $\operatorname{Sing}(u)$. This set is empty when $n\le6$, is locally finite when $n=7$, and satisfies $$\dim_{\mathrm H}\operatorname{Sing}(u)\le n-7\qquad(n\ge7).$$ For every $n\ge7$, a global minimizer attains this dimension bound.*

</div>

The upper bound is the standard dimension-reduction consequence of the critical dimension, and sharpness follows by taking Cartesian products of the De Silva–Jerison cone with Euclidean space. We verify these deductions in Section <a href="#sec:consequences" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:consequences">8</a>.

The variational theory of the one-phase Bernoulli problem begins with Alt and Caffarelli’s existence and regularity theory <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[AltCaffarelli1981](#ref-AltCaffarelli1981)]. Weiss’s monotonicity formula and dimension reduction <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Weiss1999](#ref-Weiss1999)] connect the singularities of general minimizers to homogeneous global minimizers; see also <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Velichkov2023](#ref-Velichkov2023), Theorem 1.4 and Chapters 9–10]. Thus the first dimension admitting a nonflat minimizing cone determines both the threshold for singular free boundaries and the codimension bound furnished by this theory. Our main result answers positively the critical-dimension question whether $d_*=7$.

A second strand of the theory passes from weak free boundaries to classical ones. Caffarelli’s Harnack-inequality approach established regularity for Lipschitz free boundaries <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Caffarelli1987](#ref-Caffarelli1987)]. De Silva developed a different improvement-of-flatness argument allowing a nonzero right-hand side <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[DeSilva2011](#ref-DeSilva2011), Theorems 1.1–1.2]; this is the approach presented in the regularity chapters of <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Velichkov2023](#ref-Velichkov2023)]. Higher regularity has classical antecedents in Kinderlehrer and Nirenberg <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[KinderlehrerNirenberg1977](#ref-KinderlehrerNirenberg1977), Theorem 2]. The version used here, for Lipschitz viscosity solutions, is due to De Silva, Ferrari and Salsa <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[DeSilvaFerrariSalsa2019](#ref-DeSilvaFerrariSalsa2019), Theorem 1.1 and Corollary 1.2]. These results make a classification of homogeneous minimizing blowups applicable to general local minimizers.

As recalled by Jerison and Savin <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[JerisonSavin2015](#ref-JerisonSavin2015), Introduction], the dimension-three rigidity result for stable homogeneous solutions with smooth cross-section is due to Caffarelli, Jerison and Kenig <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[CaffarelliJerisonKenig2004](#ref-CaffarelliJerisonKenig2004)]. Jerison and Savin proved that stable homogeneous solutions with smooth cross-section are flat in dimensions at most four <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[JerisonSavin2015](#ref-JerisonSavin2015), Theorem 1.1]; their regularity consequence for minimizers <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[JerisonSavin2015](#ref-JerisonSavin2015), Corollary 1.2] gives $d_*\geq5$. Their argument constructs functions of the Hessian satisfying inequalities for the linearized free-boundary problem. In the other direction, De Silva and Jerison constructed a nonflat homogeneous global minimizer in dimension seven <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[DeSilvaJerison2009](#ref-DeSilvaJerison2009), Theorem 1.1], proving $d_*\leq7$. The minimizing property in that construction is with respect to all Sobolev competitors with the prescribed boundary trace. Firester, Tsiamis and Wang also construct further symmetric minimizing one-phase cones, including a second example in dimension seven <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[FiresterTsiamisWang2026Capillary](#ref-FiresterTsiamisWang2026Capillary), Theorem 1.4].

Recent work further describes cones with symmetry. Firester, Tsiamis and Wang <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[FiresterTsiamisWang2026](#ref-FiresterTsiamisWang2026), Theorem 1.1] prove that their cohomogeneity-one cones with $O(n-k)\times O(k)$ symmetry, $1\leq k\leq n-2$, are unstable for $n\leq6$ and strictly stable for $n\geq7$. The exclusion proved here applies to globally minimizing cones without a symmetry assumption.

Cone classification also differs from rigidity of arbitrary entire stable solutions. Fernández-Real and Serra prove that every global classical stable solution in dimension four is one-dimensional <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[FernandezRealSerra2026](#ref-FernandezRealSerra2026), Theorem 1.1]. Their result removes homogeneity in that dimension.

For an admissible increasing family of nonnegative boundary data, Fernández-Real and Yu prove regularity through dimension six for almost every parameter <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[FernandezRealYu2025](#ref-FernandezRealYu2025), Assumption 1 and Theorem 1.3]. This is a generic-data result: the increase is required at a fixed linear rate on the positive set of the smaller boundary datum.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
###### The obstacle and the proof strategy.

The stability inequality compares the Dirichlet energy of a test function with a curvature term on the free boundary. The use of the harmonic Hessian has a geometric precedent in Simons’s second-fundamental-form argument for minimal cones <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[Simons1968](#ref-Simons1968), Section 6.1], a connection developed explicitly by Jerison and Savin. A natural Bernoulli test is a power of the Hessian norm. Jerison and Savin explain why this unweighted choice does not give a uniform instability criterion in dimensions four through six: the interior and boundary estimates impose different restrictions on the power, and their sharp configurations need not agree <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[JerisonSavin2015](#ref-JerisonSavin2015), Section 3.2]. Their four-dimensional argument uses a more refined function of the Hessian eigenvalues <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[JerisonSavin2015](#ref-JerisonSavin2015), Section 4].

Our proof uses the normalized shape of the Hessian to construct both a scalar test and a separate vector field whose divergence controls the test’s energy. The two constructions are chosen together, so the interior and boundary estimates retain the same tensor data. The proof has three stages.

First, suppose the first nonflat minimizing cone occurs in dimension five or six. Blowups away from its vertex reduce the dimension and are therefore flat. Regularity theory makes each component of its spherical positive set smooth, and at least one component has nonzero Hessian. A five-dimensional cone can be made independent of one extra coordinate without losing minimality. Its spherical link then has precisely two exceptional tips. Thus both possible dimensions lead to a domain $\Omega\subset S^5$ with a smooth regular boundary and, possibly, these two tips (Section <a href="#sec:reductions" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:reductions">[sec:reductions]</a>).

Second, let $g$ be the restriction of the resulting cone to the sphere, and set $T=\nabla^2g+g\mathop{\mathrm{Id}}$ and $w=|T|$. Where $w>0$, we construct a positive scalar $h$ from $w$, the normalized tensor $T/w$, $g$, and $\nabla g$, and a vector field $Z$ from the same data and $\nabla T/w$. The target inequalities are $$\mathop{\mathrm{div}}Z\ge |\nabla h|^2+4h^2+\epsilon_*w^{2a},
  \qquad N\cdot Z+Hh^2\ge0,$$ where $a\in(1/2,1)$ is fixed, $\epsilon_*>0$, $N$ points into $\Omega$, and $H=-T(N,N)$. Spherical stability supplies the reverse integrated comparison between the test’s energy and its boundary curvature term. The extra positive mass $\epsilon_*w^{2a}$ will give the contradiction. The geometry and stability inequality are developed in Section <a href="#sec:geometry" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:geometry">[sec:geometry]</a>. We then state the universal tensor inequality and derive the two geometric comparisons; the full exact verification follows in Section <a href="#sec:certificate-proof" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:certificate-proof">6</a>.

Two cancellations make this construction possible. A Laplacian contraction and an antisymmetric curvature contraction eliminate fourth derivatives from $\mathop{\mathrm{div}}Z$. At the boundary, reflection in the normal direction removes the derivative components not determined by the boundary conditions. The remaining inequalities involve only finitely many normalized tensors. We prove them by weighted sums of tensor squares and exact rational remainder bounds, with all coefficients and contraction conventions supplied in Appendix <a href="#app:tables" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="app:tables">9</a>.

Finally, neither the normalized Hessian nor its derivatives are defined at $w=0$, and suspension introduces singular tips. These sets cannot be discarded without an estimate. A regularized Bochner identity gives an integrable weight controlling the derivatives near every Hessian zero. The explicit suspension formulas give an integrable power of the distance parameter at both tips. Smooth product cutoffs therefore remove both obstructions and make all error terms vanish. Section <a href="#sec:cutoffs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:cutoffs">[sec:cutoffs]</a> completes the stability contradiction.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
###### Organization and conventions.

Section <a href="#sec:reductions" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:reductions">[sec:reductions]</a> reduces a hypothetical first nonflat minimizer to a smooth spherical component in dimension six, allowing two suspension tips. Section <a href="#sec:geometry" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:geometry">[sec:geometry]</a> derives the tensor identities and the stability inequality.
